  240|       |// For 0<=x<=1/2, alternating odd powers decrease. The remainder after
  241|       |// any partial sum has the sign of the next term and magnitude at most that
  242|       |// term. Directed interval operations enclose every term for the exact source.
  243|  18.9k|fn series(
  244|  18.9k|    argument: &DyadicInterval,
  245|  18.9k|    working: i32,
  246|  18.9k|    budget: &mut DyadicBudget,
  247|  18.9k|) -> Result<DyadicInterval, IntervalError> {
  248|  18.9k|    budget.reserve(1, 4, false)?;
                                             ^0
  249|  18.9k|    let zero = DyadicInterval::point(BigInt::zero(), 0)?;
                                                                     ^0
  250|  18.9k|    let tolerance = DyadicInterval::new(BigInt::zero(), BigInt::one(), working)?;
                                                                                             ^0
  251|  18.9k|    let ratio = argument.square(budget)?.regrid(working, budget)?;
                                                     ^0                       ^0
  252|  18.9k|    let mut power = argument.add(&zero, budget)?;
                                                             ^0
  253|  18.9k|    let mut sum = zero.clone();
  254|  18.9k|    let mut denominator = 1u64;
  255|  18.9k|    let mut subtract = false;
  256|       |    loop {
  257|   180k|        budget.reserve(u64::from(u64::BITS - denominator.leading_zeros()), 2, false)?;
                                                                                                  ^0
  258|   180k|        let term = power.div(
  259|   180k|            &DyadicInterval::point(denominator.into(), 0)?,
                                                                       ^0
  260|   180k|            working,
  261|   180k|            budget,
  262|      0|        )?;
  263|   180k|        let stop = term.subset_of(&tolerance, budget)?;
                                                                   ^0
  264|   180k|        let signed = if subtract { term.neg(budget)? } else { term };
                                                 ^87.0k^87.0k^87.0k ^0         ^93.9k

  266|  18.9k|            return sum.add(&signed.hull(&zero, budget)?, budget);

  267|   162k|        }

  270|   162k|        subtract = !subtract;

  326|       |// Exact midpoint of ordered primitive lattice endpoints. Shared by discrete
  327|       |// integer ranges and fixed-point intervals; only the numerator is returned.
  328|       |#[cfg(any(feature = "integer", feature = "fixed"))]
  329|       |macro_rules! lattice_midpoint {
  330|       |    ($lower:expr, $upper:expr, $rounding:expr, $endpoint:ty) => {{
  331|       |        let lower = $lower;
  332|       |        let upper = $upper;
  333|       |        let rounding = $rounding;
  334|       |        let width = upper.abs_diff(lower);
  335|       |        // Half of the maximum unsigned distance fits the signed
  336|       |        // endpoint, and the sum is between lower and upper.
  337|       |        // These additions cannot wrap: both rounded choices lie in the bounds.
  338|       |        let floor = lower.wrapping_add((width / 2) as $endpoint);
  339|       |        let increment = width % 2 != 0
  340|       |            && match rounding {
  341|       |                crate::MidpointRounding::Floor => false,
  342|       |                crate::MidpointRounding::Ceiling => true,
  343|       |                crate::MidpointRounding::NearestTiesEven => floor % 2 != 0,
  344|       |            };
  345|       |        floor.wrapping_add(<$endpoint>::from(increment))
  346|       |    }};
  347|       |}
  348|       |#[cfg(any(feature = "integer", feature = "fixed"))]
  349|       |pub(crate) use lattice_midpoint;

    7|       |/// Caller-owned accounting for dyadic arithmetic and directed conversions.
    8|       |///
    9|       |/// Each stage reserves an upper bound `n` on its input/output magnitude bits.
   10|       |/// Linear stages cost `n` units; product/division stages cost `n*n` units.
   11|       |/// Square-root extraction reserves `2 * bit_length(n) + 4` quadratic stages,
   12|       |/// with `n` the scaled quotient bit length plus two.
   13|       |/// The formulas deliberately overestimate small or cancelling results. A size
   14|       |/// limit bounds each reservation; a work limit bounds their cumulative cost.
   15|       |/// Both limits are checked before the corresponding allocation/arithmetic.
   16|       |/// These are deterministic accounting units, not CPU cycles, allocation bytes,
   17|       |/// or an instruction-count proof for the BigInt implementation. Caller-owned
   18|       |/// inputs, constructor normalization and Hyperreal evaluation are outside this
   19|       |/// accounting. There is no cache, and a fresh budget permits an independent retry.
   20|       |#[derive(Clone, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
   21|       |pub struct DyadicBudget {
   22|       |    max_bits: u64,
   23|       |    remaining_work: u64,
   24|       |}
   25|       |
   26|       |impl DyadicBudget {
   27|       |    /// Sets the maximum reserved magnitude bits and cumulative work units.
   28|   556k|    pub const fn new(max_bits: u64, work_units: u64) -> Self {
   29|   556k|        Self {
   30|   556k|            max_bits,
   31|   556k|            remaining_work: work_units,
   32|   556k|        }
   33|   556k|    }
   34|       |

   36|      3|    pub const fn max_bits(&self) -> u64 {

   38|      3|    }
   39|       |
   40|       |    /// Unconsumed deterministic work units.
   41|  10.9k|    pub const fn remaining_work(&self) -> u64 {
   42|  10.9k|        self.remaining_work
   43|  10.9k|    }
   44|       |

   67|       |/// An exact closed real interval `[lower, upper] * 2^exponent`, or empty.
   68|       |///
   69|       |/// Endpoints share a signed 32-bit exponent. Common powers of two are removed
   70|       |/// up to the exponent's maximum; zero and empty have canonical exponent zero.
   71|       |/// Normalization preserves the set, not the originally requested grid tag.
   72|       |/// Arithmetic is exact except explicitly outward-rounded division/regridding.
   73|       |/// Common-grid operations keep exact zero on the other operand's grid. Copying
   74|       |/// zero still consumes work, but requires no magnitude-bit shift reservation;

  565|       |    /// Exact image of a nonnegative integer power.
  566|       |    ///
  567|       |    /// Exponent zero maps every nonempty set to one, including zero; empty maps
  568|       |    /// to empty. Odd powers preserve order and even powers retain a zero minimum
  569|       |    /// when the source contains zero. For negative powers, explicitly compose
  570|       |    /// with `reciprocal` at the caller's chosen output grid.
  571|       |    ///
  572|       |    /// Each nontrivial endpoint power reserves `input_bits * exponent` bits
  573|       |    /// and at most twice the exponent's bit length in quadratic work stages
  574|       |    /// before exponentiation. Zero and unit mantissas need only constant work.
  575|       |    /// Exponents one and two reuse the existing copy and square paths.
  576|  6.39k|    pub fn checked_pow(
  577|  6.39k|        &self,
  578|  6.39k|        exponent: u32,
  579|  6.39k|        budget: &mut DyadicBudget,
  580|  6.39k|    ) -> Result<Self, IntervalError> {
  581|  6.39k|        let Some((a, b, scale)) = self.bounds() else {
                                ^6.39k^6.39k^6.39k
  582|      5|            return Ok(Self::empty());
  583|       |        };
  584|  6.39k|        match exponent {
  585|       |            0 => {
  586|    639|                budget.reserve(1, 2, false)?;
                                                         ^1
  587|    638|                return Self::point(BigInt::one(), 0);
  588|       |            }
  589|    638|            1 => return self.regrid(scale, budget),
  590|    640|            2 => return self.square(budget),
  591|  4.47k|            _ => {}
  592|       |        }
  593|  4.47k|        let aa = endpoint_power(a, exponent, budget)?;
                          ^4.47k                                  ^2
  594|  4.47k|        let bb = endpoint_power(b, exponent, budget)?;
                          ^4.47k                                  ^1
  595|  4.47k|        let (lower, upper) = if !exponent.is_multiple_of(2) {
  596|  2.55k|            (aa, bb)

  780|       |// For n >= 0 and d > 0, floor(sqrt(n/d * 2^shift)) equals
  781|       |// floor(sqrt(floor(n/d * 2^shift))). The upper endpoint equals this floor
  782|       |// exactly when the quotient is integral and its floor is a perfect square.
  783|       |// Domain checks belong to the public entry points.
  784|  18.9k|fn scaled_sqrt(
  785|  18.9k|    n: &BigInt,
  786|  18.9k|    d: &BigInt,
  787|  18.9k|    shift: i64,
  788|  18.9k|    budget: &mut DyadicBudget,
  789|  18.9k|) -> Result<(BigInt, BigInt), IntervalError> {

  792|  3.44k|        return Ok((BigInt::zero(), BigInt::zero()));
  793|  15.4k|    }
  794|  15.4k|    let (floor, ceil) = scaled_quotient(n, d, shift, budget)?;
                       ^15.4k ^15.4k                                      ^3
  795|  15.4k|    let bits = floor.bits().saturating_add(2);
  796|       |    // Charge the integer root backend logarithmically many quadratic stages,
  797|       |    // plus its exact square check, increment, and owned output. These are
  798|       |    // deterministic accounting units, not a bound on backend CPU instructions.

                                                  ^7.72k
  803|  1.37k|        lower.clone()
  804|       |    } else {
  805|  14.1k|        &lower + 1
  806|       |    };
  807|  15.4k|    Ok((lower, upper))

  812|  1.90M|pub(crate) fn scaled_quotient(
  813|  1.90M|    n: &BigInt,

    4|       |mod sealed {

    7|       |/// A sealed finite real-interval backend. Integer sets and proposals are excluded.
    8|       |///
    9|       |/// Downstream types cannot claim certified endpoint semantics through this trait.
   10|       |/// ```compile_fail

   13|       |/// impl FiniteRealInterval for Forged {
   14|       |///     fn is_empty(&self)->bool { false }
   15|       |///     fn is_singleton(&self)->bool { true }
   16|       |///     fn signs(&self)->SignSet { SignSet::Zero }
   17|       |/// }
   18|       |/// ```
   19|       |pub trait FiniteRealInterval: sealed::Sealed {
   20|       |    /// Whether the finite real set is empty.
   21|       |    fn is_empty(&self) -> bool;
   22|       |    /// Whether the finite real set contains exactly one point.
   23|       |    fn is_singleton(&self) -> bool;
   24|       |    /// Possible signs of represented real members.
   25|       |    fn signs(&self) -> SignSet;
   26|       |}

   28|       |    ($feature:literal,$ty:ty) => {

   47|       |backend!("float-enclosure", crate::F32Enclosure);

   27|       |/// Closed real interval `[lower / 2^FRAC, upper / 2^FRAC]`, or empty.
   28|       |///
   29|       |/// The endpoints are exact lattice numerators, not floating-point estimates.
   30|       |/// All constructors, including `empty`, require `FRAC < I::BITS`. The i32
   31|       |/// family includes Q2.30; i64 includes Q31.32. Storage and set relations are
   32|       |/// allocation-free `no_std`. Empty is a separate state, never reversed bounds.
   33|       |/// Serde uses null or a numerator pair and validates scale and order on import.

   61|       |        ///

   63|       |        /// finite float or between its grid points. `Ord`/`Eq` compare exact

  542|       |            /// Tight finite IEEE enclosure of an exact dyadic source interval.
  543|       |            ///
  544|       |            /// Endpoints round outward, including to zero/minimum subnormal on
  545|       |            /// underflow. Off-grid bounds widen; this is containment, not exact
  546|       |            /// equality with the borrowed source. If no finite enclosure exists,
  547|       |            /// returns Overflow instead of infinity or saturation. Empty maps to
  548|       |            /// empty without work; nonempty input requires backend qualification.
  549|       |            ///
  550|       |            /// Both endpoint conversions share the caller's DyadicBudget. Each
  551|       |            /// reserves three linear stages of max(input magnitude bits, IEEE
  552|       |            /// significand bits plus one), before limb inspection/allocation.

  554|       |            /// accounting excludes actual heap bytes and cancellation as usual.
  555|       |            /// Failure publishes no partial interval and leaves the source intact.

  250|       |    // The existing wide adder retains the subnormal tail and handles large
  251|       |    // exponent gaps. This also avoids overflowing a sum whose mean is finite.
  252|  40.5k|    pub(crate) fn half_sum(self, a: u64, b: u64) -> Result<(u64, u64), IntervalError> {
  253|  40.5k|        let mut x = self.decode(a);
  254|  40.5k|        let mut y = self.decode(b);
  255|  40.5k|        x.exponent -= 1;
  256|  40.5k|        y.exponent -= 1;
  257|  40.5k|        self.add_wide(x, y)
  258|  40.5k|    }
  259|       |
  260|       |    // Add two exact dyadics with at most 106 magnitude bits each. Choose a
  261|       |    // common work grid giving the dominant operand bit 126, leaving one carry
  262|       |    // bit. At most the smaller operand loses a fractional tail. A lost tail
  263|       |    // implies a top-bit separation of at least 21, so cancellation cannot make
  264|       |    // the result small or its IEEE spacing finer than the work grid. Therefore
  265|       |    // the tail can change the final bounds only at an exact IEEE grid point.
  266|   364k|    fn add_wide(self, x: Dyadic, y: Dyadic) -> Result<(u64, u64), IntervalError> {
  267|   364k|        if x.magnitude == 0 {
  268|  8.66k|            return self.rounded(y);
  269|   355k|        }
  270|   355k|        if y.magnitude == 0 {
  271|  3.92k|            return self.rounded(x);
  272|   351k|        }
  273|   703k|        let top = |v: Dyadic| v.exponent + 127 - v.magnitude.leading_zeros() as i32;
                          ^351k
  274|   351k|        let grid = top(x).max(top(y)) - 126;
  275|   703k|        let align = |v: Dyadic| {
                          ^351k
  276|   703k|            let shift = v.exponent - grid;
  277|   703k|            let (magnitude, lost) = if shift >= 0 {
  278|   566k|                debug_assert!(shift < 128 && v.magnitude.leading_zeros() >= shift as u32);
  279|   566k|                (v.magnitude << shift, false)
  280|   136k|            } else if -shift >= 128 {
  281|  89.6k|                (0, true)
  282|       |            } else {
  283|  47.1k|                (
  284|  47.1k|                    v.magnitude >> -shift,
  285|  47.1k|                    v.magnitude & ((1u128 << -shift) - 1) != 0,
  286|  47.1k|                )
  287|       |            };
  288|       |            (
  289|   703k|                magnitude,
  290|   703k|                if !lost {
  291|   567k|                    0
  292|   135k|                } else if v.negative {
  293|  67.1k|                    -1
  294|       |                } else {
  295|  68.7k|                    1
  296|       |                },
  297|       |            )
  298|   703k|        };
  299|   351k|        let (a, atail) = align(x);
  300|   351k|        let (b, btail) = align(y);
  301|   351k|        debug_assert!(atail == 0 || btail == 0);
                                                  ^74.4k
  302|   351k|        let (negative, magnitude) = if x.negative == y.negative {
  303|   176k|            (x.negative, a + b)
  304|   175k|        } else if a >= b {
  305|  91.0k|            (x.negative, a - b)
  306|       |        } else {
  307|  84.6k|            (y.negative, b - a)
  308|       |        };

                           ^305k^305k
  310|   351k|            negative,
  311|   351k|            magnitude,
  312|   351k|            exponent: grid,
  313|   351k|        })?;
                        ^45.8k

                                                              ^78^36.6k
  316|  37.5k|            (true, 1) => Ok((lo, self.next_up(hi)?)),
                                                               ^150
  317|   231k|            _ => Ok((lo, hi)),
  318|       |        }
  319|   364k|    }

  321|   323k|    pub(crate) fn affine(
  322|   323k|        self,
  323|   323k|        x: u64,
  324|   323k|        scale: u64,
  325|   323k|        offset: u64,
  326|   323k|    ) -> Result<(u64, u64), IntervalError> {
  327|   323k|        let x = self.decode(x);
  328|   323k|        let scale = self.decode(scale);
  329|   323k|        self.add_wide(
  330|   323k|            Dyadic {
  331|   323k|                negative: x.negative != scale.negative,
  332|   323k|                magnitude: x.magnitude * scale.magnitude,
  333|   323k|                exponent: x.exponent + scale.exponent,
  334|   323k|            },
  335|   323k|            self.decode(offset),
  336|       |        )
  337|   323k|    }

  339|   328k|    pub(crate) fn div(self, a: u64, b: u64) -> Result<(u64, u64), IntervalError> {
  340|   328k|        if b == 0 {
  341|    239|            return Err(IntervalError::ContainsZero);
  342|   328k|        }
  343|   328k|        if a == 0 {
  344|  2.31k|            return Ok((0, 0));
  345|   326k|        }
  346|   326k|        let x = self.decode(a);
  347|   326k|        let y = self.decode(b);
  348|   326k|        let mut order = y.magnitude.leading_zeros() as i32 - x.magnitude.leading_zeros() as i32;
  349|   326k|        let below = if order >= 0 {
  350|   313k|            x.magnitude < y.magnitude << order
  351|       |        } else {
  352|  13.0k|            x.magnitude << -order < y.magnitude
  353|       |        };

  355|   144k|            order -= 1;
  356|   181k|        }

  358|   326k|        let top = exponent + order;

  360|  15.5k|            return Err(IntervalError::Overflow);

  363|   310k|        let shift = exponent - grid;

  365|   281k|            debug_assert!(shift < 128 && x.magnitude.leading_zeros() >= shift as u32);
  366|   281k|            let n = x.magnitude << shift;
  367|   281k|            (n / y.magnitude, !n.is_multiple_of(y.magnitude))
  368|  29.6k|        } else if -shift > y.magnitude.leading_zeros() as i32 {
  369|  11.9k|            (0, true)
  370|       |        } else {
  371|  17.7k|            let d = y.magnitude << -shift;
  372|  17.7k|            (x.magnitude / d, !x.magnitude.is_multiple_of(d))
  373|       |        };

  375|   310k|            self.encode(floor, grid)?,
                                                  ^0
  376|   310k|            self.encode(floor + u128::from(remainder), grid)?,
                                                                          ^0
  377|   310k|            x.negative != y.negative,
  378|       |        ))
  379|   328k|    }

    1|       |//! Directed projection of arbitrary finite dyadic endpoints onto IEEE grids.
    2|       |use super::Format;
    3|       |use crate::{DyadicBudget, IntervalError};
    4|       |use num_bigint::{BigInt, Sign};
    5|       |use num_traits::ToPrimitive;
    6|       |
    7|       |impl Format {
    8|  50.6k|    pub(crate) fn enclose_dyadic(
    9|  50.6k|        self,
   10|  50.6k|        mantissa: &BigInt,
   11|  50.6k|        exponent: i32,
   12|  50.6k|        budget: &mut DyadicBudget,
   13|  50.6k|    ) -> Result<(u64, u64), IntervalError> {
   14|  50.6k|        let magnitude = mantissa.magnitude();
   15|  50.6k|        let bits = magnitude.bits();
   16|       |        // Reserve before limb scans/shifts. Three linear stages cover input
   17|       |        // inspection, retained significand and discarded-tail inspection. The
   18|       |        // carry needs at most fraction+2 bits. No exponent-sized shift occurs.
   19|  50.6k|        budget.reserve(bits.max(u64::from(self.fraction()) + 2), 3, false)?;
                                                                                        ^8
   20|  50.6k|        if bits == 0 {
   21|    144|            return Ok((0, 0));
   22|  50.4k|        }
   23|  50.4k|        let negative = mantissa.sign() == Sign::Minus;
   24|  50.4k|        let top = i128::from(exponent) + i128::from(bits) - 1;
   25|  50.4k|        if top > i128::from(self.bias()) {
   26|  2.88k|            return Err(IntervalError::Overflow);
   27|  47.5k|        }
   28|  47.5k|        if top < i128::from(self.minimum()) {
   29|  6.59k|            return Ok(self.signed(0, 1, negative));
   30|  40.9k|        }
   31|       |        // top is now between the smallest subnormal and largest normal
   32|       |        // exponents. The output grid and significand fit the existing encoder.
   33|  40.9k|        let grid = self.minimum().max(top as i32 - self.fraction() as i32);
   34|  40.9k|        let shift = i64::from(grid) - i64::from(exponent);
   35|  40.9k|        let (floor, remainder) = if shift > 0 {
   36|  12.9k|            let shift = usize::try_from(shift).map_err(|_| IntervalError::SizeExhausted)?;
                                                                                                      ^0
   37|  12.9k|            let floor = (magnitude >> shift)
   38|  12.9k|                .to_u128()
   39|  12.9k|                .expect("retained IEEE significand has at most 53 bits");
   40|  12.9k|            let remainder = magnitude.trailing_zeros().expect("nonzero magnitude") < shift as u64;
   41|  12.9k|            (floor, remainder)
   42|       |        } else {
   43|  28.0k|            let floor = magnitude
   44|  28.0k|                .to_u128()
   45|  28.0k|                .expect("unshifted IEEE significand has at most 53 bits")
   46|  28.0k|                << -shift;
   47|  28.0k|            (floor, false)
   48|       |        };
   49|  40.9k|        let lower = self.encode(floor, grid)?;
                                                          ^0
   50|  40.9k|        let upper = self.encode(floor + u128::from(remainder), grid)?;
                          ^40.9k                                                  ^68
   51|  40.9k|        Ok(self.signed(lower, upper, negative))
   52|  50.6k|    }

   15|       |pub trait IntegerEndpoint: sealed::Sealed + Copy + Ord {
   16|       |    /// Exact additive identity.
   17|       |    const ZERO: Self;
   18|       |}
   19|       |

   21|       |    ($($t:ty),*) => { $(impl IntegerEndpoint for $t { const ZERO: Self = 0; })* };
   22|       |}
   23|       |endpoints!(
   24|       |    i8, i16, i32, i64, i128, isize, u8, u16, u32, u64, u128, usize
   25|       |);
   26|       |
   27|       |/// Exact discrete integer set `{lower, ..., upper}`, or the empty set.
   28|       |///
   29|       |/// This is not a continuous real interval. Endpoints are exact primitive
   30|       |/// integers; construction rejects reversed endpoints. Storage and relations
   31|       |/// allocate nothing and require no `std`. There is no implicit midpoint or
   32|       |/// scalar order. Serde encodes null for empty and an ordered pair otherwise,
   33|       |/// rebuilding through checked construction on import.
   34|       |///
   35|       |/// Downstream endpoint implementations cannot assert certified semantics:
   36|       |///
   37|       |/// ```compile_fail
   38|       |/// use hyperinterval::IntegerEndpoint;
   39|       |/// #[derive(Clone, Copy, Eq, PartialEq, Ord, PartialOrd)]
   40|       |/// struct UserEndpoint(i32);
   41|       |/// impl IntegerEndpoint for UserEndpoint {
   42|       |///     const ZERO: Self = Self(0);
   43|       |/// }
   44|       |/// ```
   45|       |///
   46|       |/// Set relations must be named; scalar ordering is intentionally unavailable:
   47|       |///
   48|       |/// ```compile_fail
   49|       |/// use hyperinterval::IntegerRange;
   50|       |/// let a = IntegerRange::<i32>::point(1);
   51|       |/// let b = IntegerRange::<i32>::point(2);
   52|       |/// let _ = a < b;
   53|       |/// ```

   57|       |}

/mnt/development/interval-coverage-review/csgrs/crates/hyperinterval/src/integer_quotient.rs:
    1|       |use crate::IntervalError;
    2|       |
    3|       |// Every caller establishes a positive denominator. Division cannot meet
    4|       |// i128::MIN / -1. A nonzero remainder implies the quotient is below i128::MAX,
    5|       |// so the ceiling increment cannot overflow, even for full-width numerators.
    6|       |#[inline]
    7|  3.52M|pub(crate) fn positive_quotient_bounds(numerator: i128, denominator: i128) -> (i128, i128) {
    8|  3.52M|    let quotient = numerator / denominator;
    9|  3.52M|    let remainder = numerator % denominator;
   10|       |    // A negative remainder requires a downward correction; a positive one an
   11|       |    // upward correction. At either signed limit the remainder must be zero,
   12|       |    // so neither correction can wrap. This also lets callers discard an
   13|       |    // unused endpoint without retaining an impossible overflow panic path.
   14|  3.52M|    (
   15|  3.52M|        quotient.wrapping_sub(i128::from(remainder < 0)),
   16|  3.52M|        quotient.wrapping_add(i128::from(remainder > 0)),
   17|  3.52M|    )
   18|  3.52M|}
   19|       |
   20|       |/// Exact integer floor and ceiling of the rational value `numerator / denominator`.
   21|       |///
   22|       |/// Returns `(floor, ceiling)`, equal precisely for an integral quotient. These
   23|       |/// are numerical bounds on the rational value, not a claim that a discrete
   24|       |/// integer set contains a noninteger value. Neither endpoint is selected as a
   25|       |/// replacement for the exact ratio.
   26|       |///
   27|       |/// A zero denominator returns [`IntervalError::ContainsZero`]. The sole
   28|       |/// unrepresentable quotient, `i128::MIN / -1`, returns [`IntervalError::Overflow`].
   29|       |/// Both denominator signs and signed extremes are supported without allocating,
   30|       |/// changing rounding state or requiring arbitrary precision.
   31|       |///
   32|       |/// ```
   33|       |/// use hyperinterval::integer_quotient_bounds;
   34|       |/// assert_eq!(integer_quotient_bounds(-7, 3).unwrap(), (-3, -2));
   35|       |/// assert_eq!(integer_quotient_bounds(-7, -3).unwrap(), (2, 3));
   36|       |/// ```
   37|       |#[inline]
   38|  69.8k|pub fn integer_quotient_bounds(
   39|  69.8k|    numerator: i128,
   40|  69.8k|    denominator: i128,
   41|  69.8k|) -> Result<(i128, i128), IntervalError> {
   42|  69.8k|    if denominator > 0 {
   43|  34.6k|        return Ok(positive_quotient_bounds(numerator, denominator));
   44|  35.2k|    }
   45|  35.2k|    if denominator == 0 {
   46|    259|        return Err(IntervalError::ContainsZero);
   47|  34.9k|    }
   48|       |    // Do not negate a signed minimum merely to normalize the denominator.
   49|  34.9k|    let quotient = numerator
                      ^34.9k
   50|  34.9k|        .checked_div(denominator)
   51|  34.9k|        .ok_or(IntervalError::Overflow)?;
                                                     ^1
   52|  34.9k|    let inexact = numerator % denominator != 0;

/mnt/development/interval-coverage-review/csgrs/crates/hyperinterval/src/rational_admission.rs:
    1|       |//! Exact rational operations admitted before Hyperreal normalization or arithmetic.
    2|       |use crate::{DyadicBudget, IntervalError, RationalInterval};
    3|       |use core::cmp::Ordering;
    4|       |use hyperreal::Rational;
    5|       |
    6|       |/// A borrowed admission view sharing a native arithmetic counter.
    7|       |///
    8|       |/// Mathematical operations delegate to Hyperreal without changing its dispatch
    9|       |/// order. Admission inspects stored, possibly unreduced ratio parts without
   10|       |/// canonicalization. Let `s(x)` be the larger stored numerator/denominator bit
   11|       |/// count. Comparison/multiplication reserve `n=s(a)+s(b)` coefficient bits;
   12|       |/// addition/subtraction reserve one more carry bit. Comparison costs `4*n²`
   13|       |/// units and arithmetic costs `16*n²`, with n clamped to one for charging.
   14|       |/// Charges precede all delegated arithmetic and do not depend on cache warmth.
   15|       |///
   16|       |/// These are deterministic admission weights and input/result coefficient
   17|       |/// envelopes, not a count of Hyperreal's GCD steps, CPU instructions, internal
   18|       |/// temporary space or retained caches. Payload lifecycle, input construction and
   19|       |/// cache work retain their existing exclusions. This view does not establish a
   20|       |/// whole-work or total-heap bound for rational arithmetic.
   21|       |#[derive(Debug)]
   22|       |pub struct RationalArithmetic<'a> {
   23|       |    budget: &'a mut DyadicBudget,
   24|       |}
   25|       |
   26|       |impl DyadicBudget {
   27|       |    /// Borrow exact rational admission using this same remaining counter.
   28|       |    /// No fresh budget is created; subsequent dyadic conversion/arithmetic shares it.
   29|  70.8k|    pub fn rational(&mut self) -> RationalArithmetic<'_> {
   30|  70.8k|        RationalArithmetic { budget: self }
   31|  70.8k|    }
   32|       |}
   33|       |
   34|       |impl RationalArithmetic<'_> {
   35|  70.8k|    fn admit(
   36|  70.8k|        &mut self,
   37|  70.8k|        left: &Rational,
   38|  70.8k|        right: &Rational,
   39|  70.8k|        carry: bool,
   40|  70.8k|        stages: u64,
   41|  70.8k|    ) -> Result<(), IntervalError> {
   42|   141k|        let size = |value: &Rational| {
                          ^70.8k
   43|   141k|            let (_, numerator, denominator) = value.ratio_parts();
   44|   141k|            numerator.bits().max(denominator.bits())
   45|   141k|        };
   46|  70.8k|        let bits = size(left)
   47|  70.8k|            .saturating_add(size(right))
   48|  70.8k|            .saturating_add(u64::from(carry));
   49|  70.8k|        self.budget.reserve(bits, stages, true)
   50|  70.8k|    }
   51|       |
   52|       |    /// Exact total comparison after admission, with no rounded intermediate.
   53|  28.3k|    pub fn compare(
   54|  28.3k|        &mut self,
   55|  28.3k|        left: &Rational,
   56|  28.3k|        right: &Rational,

                                                       ^3
   59|  28.3k|        Ok(left
